[13/03/2026, 05:20:12] ~ Shaqir Nawfer: Hey guys, if you haven’t already done so, just a quick heads up. Due to the GPS being jammed in Dubai recently, I came across a tip on how to download offline maps.

You can do it by opening your Google Maps app → tapping your profile icon in the top right corner → selecting Offline maps → choose Select your own map → then adjusting the map range (I recommend selecting the whole UAE). Once you download it, you’ll still be able to use the map even if the signal acts up.

Hope this helps! 👍

[14/03/2026, 13:29:56] ~ M.D: A small reminder from the heart:

Many taxi drivers in the city are having a very quiet month, and many of them will only be earning around 500–700 AED in March.

If you take a Hala taxi through Careem, consider leaving a tip. Even 10 AED (I think it also works with 5 AED) can make a real difference. Careem is currently matching the tip, which means your support will double and go directly into the driver’s salary at the end of the month.

Dubai has always felt special because people here quietly take care of one another. A small gesture, a moment of kindness, sometimes that’s exactly what reminds someone that they are not alone in this city.

[17/03/2026, 02:07:03] ~ J: Inshallah allah swt answers your duas. Allah is the best of planners.
[17/03/2026, 04:45:54] ~ Tuyyabah: Wa alaikum salam.  

Did you have any written agreement or email confirmation confirming the commission and the terms of the deal? Normally these things should be agreed in writing beforehand.  

Also, were you RERA licensed and registered on the transaction through a brokerage? In Dubai, developers usually only recognise commission payments to brokers who are RERA licensed and properly registered on the deal.  

If you introduced the buyer and the sale completed because of your introduction, you could still try contacting the developer directly to clarify their position and whether you were recorded as the broker on the deal.

So firstly, I'd contact the developer's customer care team and check whether the commission was paid and how much. 

Good luck

I teach students from Year 7 through to Year 13 across KS3, IGCSE and IB English, with my exam classes consistently achieving above-average results. Whilst I have experience across all ability ranges, much of my IGCSE teaching experience has been with top sets - helping mid-range and high-achieving students develop the depth and sophistication needed to achieve the highest grades.

*I specialise in IGCSE Edexcel Pearson English Language and Literature.*

*From September, I’ll be opening a limited number of small-group classes*. Classes are capped at 5 students maximum so every child receives individual focus, personalised lesson content and detailed written feedback with clear targets to support progression.

*IGCSE English (Language & Literature)*

Weekly classes focus on:
• developing high-level analytical and essay-writing skills  
• building sophisticated vocabulary and expression  
• strengthening exam technique and structure  
• detailed knowledge of texts, themes, characters and writer’s intentions  
• helping students push into the top mark bands with confidence and clarity  

*KS3 English*

A limited number of KS3 classes (Year 7-9) will also be available to help students build strong foundations early - developing analytical thinking, writing confidence, vocabulary and the core skills that make IGCSE far more manageable later on.

One thing I hear from parents regularly is: “I wish we’d found you sooner.” English is a subject where progress builds gradually through guided feedback, consistent practice and strong foundations, which is why starting earlier can make such a significant difference.

*Some lovely feedback from students and parents:*

“From what I started to how confident I am now, I seriously can’t appreciate you more miss.”

“My teacher said I did the best in the class - she read out my answers and essay to everyone and said it was brilliant.”

“I have never seen Dhilan so enthusiastic about English. All down to you.”

I genuinely love teaching English and work hard to make lessons engaging, challenging and enjoyable whilst pushing students to think deeply, write perceptively and become confident in their abilities.

I’ll also be running a limited number of *summer booster classes* focused on strengthening the core foundations of English before the next academic year begins - building confidence with analytical thinking, essay writing, vocabulary, structure and expression so students start September feeling far more secure and prepared.
